OUTPUT: set turn="review" and return exactly one item in the "review" array; leave every other array empty.
REVIEW TURN. Independently audit EVERY candidate in `candidates`; each is a
hypothesis from the discovery turn, not a trusted conclusion. Return exactly one
decision per candidate_id.

Return obligation_class (execution_compatibility per D4, target_coverage per D5),
scope_basis, trigger_basis and the five checks of J1, each with status
established/refuted/unknown, a specific reason and exact evidence:
scope_contract (J1a, D2); feasible_trigger (J1b): for execution compatibility a
finite actual target execution, for target coverage a valid supported
behavior/setup whose route the target blocks; observable_consequence (J1c);
alternatives_ruled_out (J1d, D8), including counterevidence supplied with the
candidate; identity_preserved (J1e; for timing also T1).

Use effect blocked_target_coverage only for target_coverage. For target_coverage
fill every coverage_witness field; for the other class use empty strings. These
fields do not replace the citations of each check.

decision=keep requires all five checks established with source and harness
evidence, a finite_witness and no decisive missing fact. decision=dismiss requires
a grounded refutation or equivalent route (J6). Otherwise return uncertain and
request the missing facts (E3). For maintenance, unresolved timing does not
invalidate a currently established obligation (J2).

Then audit the whole scope again (J4): assess every selected change ID and the
four foundations with evidence, challenge both dismissals (J6) and retained
gaps (J1). Out-of-scope items (D7) are not obligations. New evidence contains
contracts retrieved for earlier requests (`evidence`); `citation_errors` lists earlier
citations the program could not verify; cite valid locations instead. Request
exact symbols or paths only for decisive missing facts (E3).
If the evidence establishes a distinct obligation that is NOT among `candidates`
(for example one that only became visible with the new evidence), report it in
`new_candidates` with the same fields as a discovery blocker and mark its change
group as blocked in the scope audit. It is reviewed in a separate turn; do not
add a decision for it here.

alloc_;\n615|\n616|  // Deallocate memory allocated by the buffer.\n617|  void deallocate() {\n618|    T* data = this->data();\n619|    if (data != store_) alloc_.deallocate(data, }\n634|\n635| private:\n636|  // Move data from other to this buffer.\n637|  void move(basic_memory_buffer& other) {\n638|    alloc_ = std::move(other.alloc_);\n639|    T* data = other.data();\n640| The idea comes from the talk by Alexandrescu\n791|    // \"Three Optimization Tips for C++\". See speed-test for a comparison.\n792|    if (n < 10) return count;\n793|    if (n < 100) return count + 1;\n794|    if (n < 1000) return count + 2;\n795|    if (n < 10000) return count + 3;\n796|    n /= 10000u;\n797|    count += 4;\n798|  }\n799|}\n800|#endif\n801|\n802|#if FMT_USE_INT128\n803|inline int count_digits(uint128_t n) {\n804|  int count = 1;\n805|  for (;;) {\n806|    // Integer division is slow so do it for a group of four digits instead\n807|    // of for every digit. 0> fp normalize(fp value);\n355|\n356|// Lower (upper) boundary is a value half way between a floating-point value\n357|// and its predecessor (successor). Boundaries have the same exponent as the\n358|// value so only significands are stored.\n359|struct boundaries {\n360|  uint64_t lower;\n361|  uint64_t upper;\n362|};\n363|\n364|// A handmade floating-point number f * pow(2, e).\n365|class fp {\n366| private:\n367|  using significand_type = uint64_t;\n368|\n369| public:\n370|  significand_type f;\n371|  int e;\n372|\n373|  // All sizes are in bits.\n374|  // Subtract 1 to account for an implicit most significant bit in the\n375|  // normalized form.\n376|  static FMT_CONSTEXPR_DECL const int double_significand_size =\n377|      std::numeric_limits<double>::digits - 1;\n378|  static FMT_CONSTEXPR_DECL const uint64_t implicit_bit =\n379|      1ULL << double_significand_size;\n380|  static FMT_CONSTEXPR_DECL const int significand_size =\n381|      bits<significand_type>::value;\n382|\n383|  fp() : f(0), e(0) {}\n384|  fp(uint64_t f_val, int e_val) : f(f_val), e(e_val) {}\n385|\n386|  // Constructs fp from an IEEE754 double. It is a template to prevent compile\n387|  // errors on platforms where double is not IEEE754.\n388|  template <typename Double> explicit fp(Double d) { assign(d); }\n389|\n390|  // Assigns d to this and return true iff predecessor is closer than successor.\n391|  template <typename Double, FMT_ENABLE_IF(sizeof(Double) == sizeof(uint64_t))>\n392|  bool assign(Double d) {\n393|    // Assume double is in the format [sign][exponent][significand].\n394|    using limits = std::numeric_limits<Double>;\n395|    const int exponent_size =\n396|        bits<Double>::value - double_significand_size - 1;  // -1 for sign\n397|    const uint64_t significand_mask = implicit_bit - 1;\n398|    const uint64_t exponent_mask = (~0ULL >> 1) & ~significand_mask;\n399|    const int exponent_bias = (1 << exponent_size) - limits::max_exponent - 1;\n400|    auto u = bit_cast<uint64_t>(d);\n401|    f = u & significand_mask;\n402|    int biased_e =\n403|        static_cast<int>((u & exponent_mask) >> double_significand_size);\n404|    // Predecessor is closer if d is a normalized power of 2 (f == 0) other than\n405|    // the smallest normalized number (biased_e > 1).\n406|    bool is_predecessor_closer = f == 0 && biased_e > 1;\n407|    if (biased_e != 0)\n408|      f += implicit_bit;\n409|    else\n410|      biased_e = 1;  // Subnormals use biased exponent 1 (min exponent).\n411|    e = biased_e - exponent_bias - double_significand_size;\n412|    return is_predecessor_closer;\n413|  }\n414|\n415|  template <typename Double, FMT_ENABLE_IF(sizeof(Double) != sizeof(uint64_t))>\n416|  bool assign(Double) {\n417|    *this = fp();\n418|    return false;\n419|  }\n420|\n421|  // Assigns d to this together with computing lower and upper boundaries,\n422|  // where a boundary is a value half way between the number and its predecessor\n423|  // (lower) or successor (upper). The upper boundary is normalized and lower\n424|  // has the same exponent but may be not normalized.\n425|  template <typename Double> boundaries assign_with_boundaries(Double d) {\n426|    bool is_lower_closer = assign(d);\n427|    fp lower =\n428|        is_lower_closer ? fp((f << 2) - 1, e - 2) : fp((f << 1) - 1, e - 1);\n429|    // 1 in normalize accounts for the exponent shift above.\n430|    fp upper = normalize<1>(fp((f << 1) + 1, e - 1));\n431|    lower.f <<= lower.e - upper.e;\n432|    return boundaries{lower.f, upper.f};\n433|  }\n434|\n435|  template <typename Double> boundaries assign_float_with_boundaries(Double d) {\n436|    assign(d);\n437|    constexpr int min_normal_e = std::numeric_limits<float>::min_exponent -\n438|                                 std::numeric_limits<double>::digits;\n439|    significand_type half_ulp = 1 << (std::numeric_limits<double>::digits -\n440|                                      std::numeric_limits<float>::digits - 1);\n441|    if (min_normal_e > e) half_ulp <<= min_normal_e - e;\n442|    fp upper = normalize<0>(fp(f + half_ulp, e));\n443|    fp lower = fp(\n444|        f - (half_ulp >> ((f == implicit_bit && e > min_normal_e) ? 1 : 0)), e);\n445|    lower.f <<= lower.e - upper.e;\n446|    return boundaries{lower.f, upper.f};\n447|  }\n448|};\n449|\n450|// Normalizes the value converted from double and multiplied by (1 << SHIFT).\n451|template <int SHIFT> fp normalize(fp value) {\n452|  // Handle subnormals.\n453|  const auto shifted_implicit_bit = fp::implicit_bit << SHIFT;\n454|  while ((value.f & shifted_implicit_bit) == 0) {\n455|    value.f <<= 1;\n456|    --value.e;\n457|  }\n458|  // Subtract 1 to account for hidden bit.\n459|  const auto offset =\n460|      fp::significand_size - fp::double_significand_size - SHIFT - 1;\n461|  value.f <<= offset;\n462|  value.e -= offset;\n463|  return value;\n464|}\n465|\n466|inline bool operator==(fp x, fp y) { return x.f == y.f && x.e == y.e; }\n467|\n468|// Computes lhs * rhs / pow(2, 64) rounded to nearest with half-up tie breaking.\n469|inline uint64_t multiply(uint64_t lhs, uint64_t rhs) {\n470|#if FMT_USE_INT128\n471|  auto product = static_cast<__uint128_t>(lhs) * rhs;\n472|  auto f = static_cast<uint64_t>(product >> 64);\n473|  return (static_cast<uint64_t>(product) & (1ULL << 63)) != 0 ? f + 1 : f;\n474|#else\n475|  // Multiply 32-bit parts of significands.\n476|  uint64_t mask = (1ULL << 32) - 1;\n477|  uint64_t a = lhs >> 32, b = lhs & mask;\n478|  uint64_t c = rhs >> 32, d = rhs & mask;\n479|  uint64_t ac = a * c, bc = b * c, ad = a * d, bd = b * d;\n480|  // Compute mid 64-bit of result and round.\n481|  uint64_t mid = (bd >> 32) + (ad & mask) + (bc & mask) + (1U << 31);\n482|  return ac + (ad >> 32) + (bc >> 32) + (mid >> 32);\n483|#endif\n484|}\n485|\n486|inline fp operator*(fp x, fp y) { return {multiply(x.f, y.f), x.e + y.e + 64}; }\n487|\n488|// Returns a cached power of 10 `c_k = c_k.f * pow(2, c_k.e)` such that its\n489|// (binary) exponent satisfies `min_exponent <= c_k.e <= min_exponent + 28`.\n490|inline fp get_cached_power(int min_exponent, int& pow10_exponent) {\n491|  const int64_t one_over_log2_10 = 0x4d104d42;  // round(pow(2, 32) / log2(10))\n492|  int index = static_cast<int>(\n493|      ((min_exponent + fp::significand_size - 1) * one_over_log2_10 +\n494|       ((int64_t(1) << 32) - 1))  // ceil\n495|      >> 32                       // arithmetic shift\n496|  );\n497|  // Decimal exponent of the first (smallest) cached power of 10.\n498|  const int first_dec_exp = -348;\n499|  // Difference between 2 consecutive decimal exponents in cached powers of 10.\n500|  const int dec_exp_step = 8;\n501|  index = (index - first_dec_exp - 1) / dec_exp_step + 1;\n502|  pow10_exponent = first_dec_exp + index * dec_exp_step;\n503|  return {data::pow10_significands[index], data::pow10_exponents[index]};\n504|}\n505|\n506|// A simple accumulator to hold the sums of terms in bigint::square if uint128_t\n507|// is not available.\n508|struct accumulator {\n509|  uint64_t lower;\n510|  uint64_t upper;\n511|\n512|  accumulator() : lower(0), upper(0) {}\n513|  explicit operator uint32_t() const { return static_cast<uint32_t>(lower); }\n514|\n515|  void operator+=(uint64_t n) {\n516|    lower += n;\n517|    if (lower < n) ++upper;\n518|  }\n519|  void operator>>=(int shift) {\n520|    assert(shift == 32);\n521|    (void)shift;\n522|    lower = (upper << 32) | (lower >> 32);\n523|    upper >>= 32;\n524|  }\n525|};\n526|\n527|class bigint {\n528| private:\n529|  // A bigint is stored as an array of bigits (big digits), with bigit at index\n530|  // 0 being the least significant one.\n531|  using bigit = uint32_t;\n532|  using double_bigit = uint64_t;\n533|  enum { bigits_capacity = 32 };\n534|  basic_memory_buffer<bigit, bigits_capacity> bigits_;\n535|  int exp_;\n536|\n537|  bigit operator[](int index) const { return bigits_[to_unsigned(index)]; bigit_index < num_bigits; ++bigit_index) {\n714|      // Compute bigit at position bigit_index of the result by adding\n715|      // cross-product terms n[i] * n[j] such that i + j == bigit_index.\n716|      for (int i = 0, j = bigit_index; j >= 0; ++i, --j) {\n717|        // Most terms are multiplied twice which can be optimized in the future.\n718|        sum += static_cast<double_bigit>(n[i]) * n[j];\n719|      }\n720|      (*this)[bigit_index] = static_cast<bigit>(sum);\n721|      sum >>= bits<bigit>::value;  // divisor - remainder won't overflow.\n770|  FMT_ASSERT(error < divisor, \"\");      // divisor - error won't overflow.\n771|  FMT_ASSERT(error < divisor - error, \"\");  // error * 2 won't overflow.\n772|  // Round down if (remainder + error) * 2 <= divisor.\n773|  if (remainder <= divisor - remainder && error * 2 <= divisor - remainder * 2)\n774|    return round_direction::down;\n775|  // Round up if (remainder - error) * 2 >= divisor.\n776|  if (remainder >= error &&\n777|      remainder - error >= divisor - (remainder - error)) {\n778|    return round_direction::up;\n779|  }\n780|  return round_direction::unknown;\n781|}\n782|\n783|namespace digits {\n784|enum result {\n785|  more,  // Generate more digits.\n786|  done,  // Done generating digits.\n787|  error  // Digit generation cancelled due to an error.\n788|};\n789|}\n790|\n791|// A version of count_digits optimized for grisu_gen_digits.\n792|inline int grisu_count_digits(uint32_t n) {\n793|  if (n < 10) return 1;\n794|  if (n < 100) return 2;\n795|  if (n < 1000) return 3;\n796|  if (n < 10000) return 4;\n797|  if (n < 100000) return 5;\n798|  if (n < 1000000) return 6;\n799|  if (n < 10000000) return 7;\n800|  if (n < 100000000) return 8;\n801|  if (n < 1000000000) return 9;\n802|  return 10;\n803|}\n804|\n805|// Generates output using the Grisu digit-gen algorithm.\n806|// error: the size of the region (lower, upper) outside of which numbers\n807|// definitely do not round to value (Delta in Grisu3).\n808|template <typename Handler>\n809|FMT_ALWAYS_INLINE digits::result grisu_gen_digits(fp value, uint64_t error,\n810|                                                  int& exp, Handler& handler) {\n811|  const fp one(1ULL << -value.e, value.e);\n812|  // The integral part of scaled value (p1 in Grisu) = value / one. It cannot be\n813|  // zero because it contains a product of two 64-bit numbers with MSB set (due\n814|  // to normalization) - 1, shifted right by at most 60 bits.\n815|  auto integral = static_cast<uint32_t>(value.f >> -one.e);\n816|  FMT_ASSERT(integral != 0, \"\");\n817|  FMT_ASSERT(integral == value.f >> -one.e, \"\");\n818|  // The fractional part of scaled value (p2 in Grisu) c = value % one.\n819|  uint64_t fractional = value.f & (one.f - 1);\n820|  exp = grisu_count_digits(integral);  // kappa in Grisu.\n821|  // Divide by 10 to prevent overflow.\n822|  auto result = handler.on_start(data::powers_of_10_64[exp - 1] << -one.e,\n823|                                 value.f / 10, error * 10, exp);\n824|  if (result != digits::more) return result;\n825|  // Generate digits for the integral part. This can produce up to 10 digits.\n826|  do {\n827|    uint32_t digit = 0;\n828|    auto divmod_integral = [&](uint32_t divisor) {\n829|      digit = integral / divisor;\n830|      integral %= divisor;\n831|    };\n832|    // This optimization by Milo Yip reduces the number of integer divisions by\n833|    // one per iteration.\n834|    switch (exp) {\n835|    case 10:\n836|      divmod_integral(1000000000);\n837|      break;\n838|    case 9:\n839|      divmod_integral(100000000);\n840|      break;\n841|    case 8:\n842|      divmod_integral(10000000);\n843|      break;\n844|    case 7:\n845|      divmod_integral(1000000);\n846|      break;\n847|    case 6:\n848|      divmod_integral(100000);\n849|      break;\n850|    case 5:\n851|      divmod_integral(10000);\n852|      break;\n853|    case 4:\n854|      divmod_integral(1000);\n855|      break;\n856|    case 3:\n857|      divmod_integral(100);\n858|      break;\n859|    case 2:\n860|      divmod_integral(10);\n861|      break;\n862|    case 1:\n863|      digit = integral;\n864|      integral = 0;\n865|      break;\n866|    default:\n867|      FMT_ASSERT(false, \"invalid number of digits\");\n868|    }\n869|    --exp;\n870|    uint64_t remainder =\n871|        (static_cast<uint64_t>(integral) << -one.e) + fractional;\n872|    result = handler.on_digit(static_cast<char>('0' + digit),\n873|                              data::powers_of_10_64[exp] << -one.e, remainder,\n874|                              error, exp, true);\n875|    if (result != digits::more) return result;\n876|  } while (exp > 0);\n877|  // Generate digits for the fractional part.\n878|  for (;;) {\n879|    fractional *= 10;\n880|    error *= 10;\n881|    char digit =\n882|        static_cast<char>('0' + static_cast<char>(fractional >> -one.e));\n883|    fractional &= one.f - 1;\n884|    --exp;\n885|    result = handler.on_digit(digit, one.f, fractional, error, exp, false);\n886|    if (result != digits::more) return result;\n887|  }\n888|}\n889|\n890|// The fixed precision digit handler.\n891|struct fixed_handler {\n892|  char* buf;\n893|  int size;\n894|  int precision;\n895|  int exp10;\n896|  bool fixed;\n897|\n898|  digits::result on_start(uint64_t divisor, uint64_t remainder, uint64_t error,\n899|                          int& exp) {\n900|    // Non-fixed formats require at least one digit and no precision adjustment.\n901|    if (!fixed) return digits::more;\n902|    // Adjust fixed precision by exponent because it is relative to decimal\n903|    // point.\n904|    precision += exp + exp10;\n905|    // Check if precision is satisfied just by leading zeros, e.g.\n906|    // format(\"{:.2f}\", 0.001) gives \"0.00\" without generating any digits.\n907|    if (precision > 0) return digits::more;\n908|    if (precision < 0) return digits::done;\n909|    auto dir = get_round_direction(divisor, remainder, error);\n910|    if (dir == round_direction::unknown) return digits::error;\n911|    buf[size++] = dir == round_direction::up ? 1 : data::powers_of_10_64[-exp];\n972|    uint64_t up = (diff - 1) * unit;  // wp_Wup\n973|    round(up, divisor, remainder, error);\n974|    uint64_t down = (diff + 1) * unit;  // wp_Wdown\n975|    if (remainder < down && error - remainder >= divisor &&\n976|        (remainder + divisor < down ||\n977|         down - remainder > remainder + divisor - down)) {\n978|      return digits::error;\n979|    }\n980|    return 2 * unit <= remainder && remainder <= error - 4 * unit\n981|               ? This eliminates multiplication\n1000|  // by 2 during later computations.\n1001|  // TODO: handle float\n1002|  int shift = value.assign(d) ? 2 : 1;\n1003|  uint64_t significand = value.f << shift;\n1004|  if (value.e >= 0) {\n1005|    numerator.assign(significand);\n1006|    numerator <<= value.e;\n1007|    lower.assign(1);\n1008|    lower <<= value.e;\n1009|    if (shift != 1) {\n1010|      upper_store.assign(1);\n1011|      upper_store <<= value.e + 1;\n1012|      upper = &upper_store;\n1013|    }\n1014|    denominator.assign_pow10(exp10);\n1015|    denominator <<= 1;\n1016|  } else if (exp10 < 0) {\n1017|    numerator.assign_pow10(-exp10);\n1018|    lower.assign(numerator);\n1019|    if (shift != 1) {\n1020|      upper_store.assign(numerator);\n1021|      upper_store <<= 1;\n1022|      upper = &upper_store;\n1023|    }\n1024|    numerator *= significand;\n1025|    denominator.assign(1);\n1026|    denominator <<= shift - value.e;\n1027|  } else {\n1028|    numerator.assign(significand);\n1029|    denominator.assign_pow10(exp10);\n1030|    denominator <<= shift - value.e;\n1031|    lower.assign(1);\n1032|    if (shift != 1) {\n1033|      upper_store.assign(1ULL << 1);\n1034|      upper = &upper_store;\n1035|    }\n1036|  }\n1037|  if (!upper) upper = &lower;\n1038|  // Invariant: value == (numerator / denominator) * pow(10, exp10).\n1039|  bool even = (value.f & 1) == 0;\n1040|  int num_digits = 0;\n1041|  char* data = buf.data();\n1042|  for (;;) {\n1043|    int digit = numerator.divmod_assign(denominator);\n1044|    bool low = compare(numerator, lower) - 1];\n1051|      } else if (high) {\n1052|        int result = add_compare(numerator, numerator, denominator);\n1053|        // Round half to even.\n1054|        if (result > 0 || (result == 0 && (digit % 2) != 0))\n1055|          ++data[num_digits - 1];\n1056|      }\n1057|      buf.resize(to_unsigned(num_digits));\n1058|      exp10 -= num_digits - 1;\n1059|      return;\n1060|    }\n1061|    numerator *= 10;\n1062|    lower *= 10;\n1063|    if (upper != &lower) *upper *= 10;\n1064|  }\n1065|}\n1066|\n1067|// Formats value using the Grisu algorithm\n1068|// (https://www.cs.tufts.edu/~nr/cs257/archive/florian-loitsch/printf.pdf)\n1069|// if T is a IEEE754 binary32 or binary64 and snprintf otherwise.\n1070|template <typename T>\n1071|int format_float(T value, int precision, float_specs specs, buffer<char>& buf) {\n1072|  static_assert(!std::is_same<T, float>::value, \"\");\n1073|  FMT_ASSERT(value >= 0, \"value is negative\");\n1074|\n1075|  const bool fixed = specs.format == float_format::fixed;\n1076|  if (value <= 0) {  // <= instead of == to silence a warning.\n1077|    if (precision <= 0 || !fixed) {\n1078|      buf.push_back('0');\n1079|      return 0;\n1080|    }\n1081|    buf.resize(to_unsigned(precision));\n1082|    std::uninitialized_fill_n(buf.data(), precision, '0');\n1083|    return -precision;\n1084|  }\n1085|\n1086|  if (!specs.use_grisu) return snprintf_float(value, precision, specs, buf);\n1087|\n1088|  int exp = 0;\n1089|  const int min_exp = -60;  // alpha in Grisu.\n1090|  int cached_exp10 = 0;     // K in Grisu.\n1091|  if (precision < 0) {\n1092|    fp fp_value;\n1093|    auto boundaries = specs.binary32\n1094|                          ? fp_value.assign_float_with_boundaries(value)\n1095|                          : fp_value.assign_with_boundaries(value);\n1096|    fp_value = normalize(fp_value);\n1097|    // Find a cached power of 10 such that multiplying value by it will bring\n1098|    // the exponent in the range [min_exp, -32].\n1099|    const fp cached_pow = get_cached_power(\n1100|        min_exp - (fp_value.e + fp::significand_size), cached_exp10);\n1101|    // Multiply value and boundaries by the cached power of 10.\n1102|    fp_value = fp_value * cached_pow;\n1103|    boundaries.lower = multiply(boundaries.lower, cached_pow.f);\n1104|    boundaries.upper = multiply(boundaries.upper, cached_pow.f);\n1105|    assert(min_exp <= fp_value.e && fp_value.e <= -32);\n1106|    --boundaries.lower;  // \\tilde{M}^- - 1 ulp -> M^-_{\\downarrow}.\n1107|    ++boundaries.upper;  // \\tilde{M}^+ + 1 ulp -> M^+_{\\uparrow}.\n1108|    // Numbers outside of (lower, upper) definitely do not round to value.\n1109|    grisu_shortest_handler handler{buf.data(), 0,\n1110|                                   boundaries.upper - fp_value.f};\n1111|    auto result =\n1112|        grisu_gen_digits(fp(boundaries.upper, fp_value.e),\n1113|                         boundaries.upper - boundaries.lower, exp, handler);\n1114|    if (result == digits::error) {\n1115|      exp += handler.size - cached_exp10 - 1;\n1116|      fallback_format(value, buf, exp);\n1117|      return exp;\n1118|    }\n1119|    buf.resize(to_unsigned(handler.size));\n1120|  } else {\n1121|    if (precision > 17) return snprintf_float(value, precision, specs, buf);\n1122|    fp normalized = normalize(fp(value));\n1123|    const auto cached_pow = get_cached_power(\n1124|        min_exp - (normalized.e + fp::significand_size), cached_exp10);\n1125|    normalized = normalized * cached_pow;\n1126|    fixed_handler handler{buf.data(), 0, precision, -cached_exp10, fixed};\n1127|    if (grisu_gen_digits(normalized, 1, exp, handler) == digits::error)\n1128|      return snprintf_float(value, precision, specs, buf);\n1129|    int num_digits = handler.size;\n1130|    if (!fixed) {\n1131|      // Remove trailing zeros.\n1132|      while (num_digits > 0 && buf[num_digits - 1] == '0') {\n1133|        --num_digits;\n1134|        ++exp;\n1135|      }\n1136|    }\n1137|    buf.resize(to_unsigned(num_digits));\n1138|  }\n1139|  return exp - cached_exp10;\n1140|}\n1141|\n1142|template <typename T>\n1143|int snprintf_float(T value, int precision, float_specs specs,\n1144|                   buffer<char>& buf) {\n1145|  // Buffer capacity must be non-zero, otherwise MSVC's vsnprintf_s will fail.\n1146|  FMT_ASSERT(buf.capacity() > buf.size(), \"empty buffer\");\n1147|  static_assert(!std::is_same<T, float>::value, \"\");\n1148|\n1149|  // Subtract 1 to account for the difference in precision since we use %e for\n1150|  // both general and exponent format.\n1151|  if (specs.format == float_format::general ||\n1152|      specs.format == float_format::exp)\n1153|    precision = (precision >= 0 ? precision : 6) - 1;\n1154|\n1155|  // Build the format string.\n1156|  enum { max_format_size = 7 };  // The longest format is \"%#.*Le\".\n1157|  char format[max_format_size];\n1158|  char* format_ptr = format;\n1159|  *format_ptr++ = '%';\n1160|  if (specs.showpoint && specs.format == float_format::hex) *format_ptr++ = '#';\n1161|  if (precision >= 0) {\n1162|    *format_ptr++ = '.';\n1163|    *format_ptr++ = '*';\n1164|  }\n1165|  if (std::is_same<T, long double>()) *format_ptr++ = 'L';\n1166|  *format_ptr++ = specs.format != float_format::hex\n1167|                      ? This\n1251| * means the buffer _must_ have at least three bytes of zero padding\n1252| * following the end of the data stream.\n1253| *\n1254| * Errors are reported in e, which will be non-zero if the parsed\n1255| * character was somehow invalid: invalid byte sequence, non-canonical\n1256| * encoding, or a surrogate half.\n1257| *\n1258| * The function returns a pointer to the next character. 0x1b) << 7;  // surrogate half?\n1290|  *e |= (*c > 0x10FFFF) << 8;       // out of range?\n1291|  *e |= (s[1] & 0xc0) >> 2;\n1292|  *e |= (s[2] & 0xc0) >> 4;\n1293|  *e |= (s[3]) >> 6;\n1294|  *e ^=
